An outline on an SVG rect is painted around the\n         element's box in the viewport, where the row's neighbours and the\n         diagram's edges cut it: the reporter saw a ring with only its top\n         and left sides left. A stroke is part of the drawing, so all four\n         sides are visible, and the rect is inset far enough to keep them\n         so: it starts 4 units inside the box, which is 2 clear of the\n         contour at x+2, and is 2 units shorter than the 17-unit row\n         pitch, which leaves 1 unit of air above and below. That guess was made when the dialog was a header, a legend\n         and a chart; it never grew with the override banner, the delta row,\n         the third button, the status line or the Temperatures section. Once\n         the real chrome passed the budget the content ran past the dialog's\n         painted background instead of being contained -- 449px past it at\n         1400x700, measured.\n\n         Worse than a stale number, it was a feedback loop: a shorter viewport\n         made the dialog *wider*, and _scaleDialogFont derives the font every\n         piece of chrome is sized from off the measured width, so the chrome\n         grew in the same direction as the overflow.\n\n         So the height budget is gone. A stroke is part of the drawing, so all four sides are visible, and the rect is inset far enough to keep them so: it starts 4 units inside the box, which is 2 clear of the contour at x+2, and is 2 units shorter than the 17-unit row pitch, which leaves 1 unit of air above and below. The width used to be min(96vw, calc((100vh - 168px) * RATIO)), where 168px was a hardcoded guess at how tall the chrome around the chart would be. That guess was made when the dialog was a header, a legend and a chart; it never grew with the override banner, the delta row, the third button, the status line or the Temperatures section. Once the real chrome passed the budget the content ran past the dialog's painted background instead of being contained -- 449px past it at 1400x700, measured. Worse than a stale number, it was a feedback loop: a shorter viewport made the dialog *wider*, and _scaleDialogFont derives the font every piece of chrome is sized from off the measured width, so the chrome grew in the same direction as the overflow. So the height budget is gone. The dialog is a flex column bounded by the viewport; the header and legend keep their natural height and everything below them scrolls. The width is still tied to viewport height, but only to keep the chart a sensible size -- nothing depends on guessing the chrome, and being wrong now costs a scrollbar rather than spilled content. The chart keeps its exact aspect ratio deliberately. It is drawn with preserveAspectRatio=\"none\", so constraining its height instead would stretch every axis label horizontally. */ dialog.expanded { box-sizing: border-box; width: min(96vw, calc(78vh * 2.3684210526315788), 1700px); max-width: 96vw; /* vh is the *large* viewport: on a phone it includes the strip behind a retracted URL bar, so a dialog sized in vh can sit partly under browser chrome. dvh tracks what is actually visible. The width used to be min(96vw, calc((100vh - 168px) * RATIO)), where 168px was a hardcoded guess at how tall the chrome around the chart would be. That guess was made when the dialog was a header, a legend and a chart; it never grew with the override banner, the delta row, the third button, the status line or the Temperatures section. Once the real chrome passed the budget the content ran past the dialog's painted background instead of being contained -- 449px past it at 1400x700, measured. Worse than a stale number, it was a feedback loop: a shorter viewport made the dialog *wider*, and _scaleDialogFont derives the font every piece of chrome is sized from off the measured width, so the chrome grew in the same direction as the overflow. So the height budget is gone. The dialog is a flex column bounded by the viewport; the header and legend keep their natural height and everything below them scrolls. The width is still tied to viewport height, but only to keep the chart a sensible size -- nothing depends on guessing the chrome, and being wrong now costs a scrollbar rather than spilled content. The chart keeps its exact aspect ratio deliberately. Prose, so it wraps like\n         tt-reason. It comes last in the box, and on a chart too short for\n         all of it fitWhy drops its last lines rather than letting the box\n         run out of the chart or clipping text. */\n      .tooltip .tt-why {\n        margin-top: 4px; padding-top: 4px;\n        border-top: 1px solid var(--hpo-divider, #e0e0e0);\n        color: var(--hpo-text-2, #727272);\n        white-space: normal;\n        max-width: 260px;\n      }\n      .tooltip .tt-why .why-head { color: var(--hpo-text, #212121); }\n      .tooltip .tt-why ul { margin: 2px 0; padding-left: 1.1em; }\n      .tooltip .dot {\n        width: 8px; height: 8px; border-radius: 50%; display: inline-block;\n      }\n\n      /* Expanded view.\n\n         The width used to be min(96vw, calc((100vh - 168px) * RATIO)), where\n         168px was a hardcoded guess at how tall the chrome around the chart\n         would be. That guess was made when the dialog was a header, a legend\n         and a chart; it never grew with the override banner, the delta row,\n         the third button, the status line or the Temperatures section. Once\n         the real chrome passed the budget the content ran past the dialog's\n         painted background instead of being contained -- 449px past it at\n         1400x700, measured.\n\n         Worse than a stale number, it was a feedback loop: a shorter viewport\n         made the dialog *wider*, and _scaleDialogFont derives the font every\n         piece of chrome is sized from off the measured width, so the chrome\n         grew in the same direction as the overflow.\n\n         So the height budget is gone. The dialog is a flex column bounded by\n         the viewport; the header and legend keep their natural height and\n         everything below them scrolls. The width is still tied to viewport\n         height, but only to keep the chart a sensible size -- nothing depends\n         on guessing the chrome, and being wrong now costs a scrollbar rather\n         than spilled content.\n\n         The chart keeps its exact aspect ratio deliberately. It is drawn with\n         preserveAspectRatio=\"none\", so constraining its height instead would\n         stretch every axis label horizontally. */\n      dialog.expanded {\n        box-sizing: border-box;\n        width: min(96vw, calc(78vh * 2.3684210526315788), 1700px);\n        max-width: 96vw;\n        /* vh is the *large* viewport: on a phone it includes the strip behind\n           a retracted URL bar, so a dialog sized in vh can sit partly under\n           browser chrome. dvh tracks what is actually visible. The vh line is\n           the fallback for engines without dvh, and is no worse than the\n           unbounded height this replaced. */\n        max-height: 92vh;\n        max-height: 92dvh;\n        display: flex;\n        flex-direction: column;\n        border: none; border-radius: 12px; padding: 16px;\n        background: var(--hpo-surface, #ffffff);\n        color: var(--hpo-text, #212121);\n        box-shadow: 0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.35);\n        overflow: hidden;\n      }\n      .dlg-body {\n        flex: 1 1 auto;\n        min-height: 0;\n        overflow-y: auto;\n        /* R7-D4-01 (#1454): a width:100% table whose min-content width\n           exceeds this box overflows it, and hidden put that overflow\n           outside every gesture -- the savings table's right-aligned percent\n           column was the instance, painted where no scroll could reach it.\n           auto reaches it instead, and costs nothing at a width where\n           nothing overflows. */\n        overflow-x: auto;\n        /* Room for the scrollbar so it never lands on top of the chart. */\n        scrollbar-gutter: stable;\n      }\n      dialog.expanded::backdrop {\n        background: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55);\n      }\n      .dlg-head {\n        display: flex; A stroke is part of the drawing, so all four\n         sides are visible, and the rect is inset far enough to keep them\n         so: it starts 4 units inside the box, which is 2 clear of the\n         contour at x+2, and is 2 units shorter than the 17-unit row\n         pitch, which leaves 1 unit of air above and below. At\n         stroke-width 2, centred on the path, the ring needs exactly 1 of\n         those units on each side and touches nothing.\n         It is a :focus-visible rule, not :focus, so a mouse click leaves\n         no ring behind while a keyboard user keeps one. */\n      .setup-hit:focus-visible {\n        outline: none;\n        fill: var(--hpo-primary, #03a9f4); fill-opacity: 0.12;\n        stroke: var(--hpo-primary, #03a9f4); stroke-width: 2;\n      }\n      .headline {\n        display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 2px;\n        padding: 0 4px 8px 4px;\n      }\n      /* The savings and score stay (CLAUDE.md: never delete working\n         functionality merely to fit), drawn in the tile style above. */\n      .hl-stats {\n        display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr));\n        gap: var(--hpo-space-2, 8px);\n      }\n      .hl-label {\n        display: block; font-size: var(--hpo-text-xs, 12px);\n        color: var(--hpo-text-2, #727272);\n      }\n      .hl-value {\n        display: block; margin-top: 2px; font-size: var(--hpo-text-md, 15px);\n        font-weight: 600; font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;\n        color: var(--hpo-text, #212121); overflow-wrap: break-word;\n      }\n      /* D4-06: the always-visible form of the baseline qualifier that used\n         to live only in the hover title. That guess was made when the dialog was a header, a legend\n         and a chart; it never grew with the override banner, the delta row,\n         the third button, the status line or the Temperatures section. Once\n         the real chrome passed the budget the content ran past the dialog's\n         painted background instead of being contained -- 449px past it at\n         1400x700, measured.\n\n         Worse than a stale number, it was a feedback loop: a shorter viewport\n         made the dialog *wider*, and _scaleDialogFont derives the font every\n         piece of chrome is sized from off the measured width, so the chrome\n         grew in the same direction as the overflow.\n\n         So the height budget is gone. The dialog is a flex column bounded by\n         the viewport; the header and legend keep their natural height and\n         everything below them scrolls. The width is still tied to viewport\n         height, but only to keep the chart a sensible size -- nothing depends\n         on guessing the chrome, and being wrong now costs a scrollbar rather\n         than spilled content.\n\n         The chart keeps its exact aspect ratio deliberately. An outline on an SVG rect is painted around the element's box in the viewport, where the row's neighbours and the diagram's edges cut it: the reporter saw a ring with only its top and left sides left. A stroke is part of the drawing, so all four sides are visible, and the rect is inset far enough to keep them so: it starts 4 units inside the box, which is 2 clear of the contour at x+2, and is 2 units shorter than the 17-unit row pitch, which leaves 1 unit of air above and below. At stroke-width 2, centred on the path, the ring needs exactly 1 of those units on each side and touches nothing. It is a :focus-visible rule, not :focus, so a mouse click leaves no ring behind while a keyboard user keeps one. */ .setup-hit:focus-visible { outline: none; fill: var(--hpo-primary, #03a9f4); fill-opacity: 0.12; stroke: var(--hpo-primary, #03a9f4); stroke-width: 2; } .headline { display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 2px; padding: 0 4px 8px 4px; } /* The savings and score stay (CLAUDE.md: never delete working functionality merely to fit), drawn in the tile style above. */ .hl-stats { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr)); gap: var(--hpo-space-2, 8px); } .hl-label { display: block; font-size: var(--hpo-text-xs, 12px); color: var(--hpo-text-2, #727272); } .hl-value { display: block; margin-top: 2px; font-size: var(--hpo-text-md, 15px); font-weight: 600; font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; color: var(--hpo-text, #212121); overflow-wrap: break-word; } /* D4-06: the always-visible form of the baseline qualifier that used to live only in the hover title. The width used to be min(96vw, calc((100vh - 168px) * RATIO)), where 168px was a hardcoded guess at how tall the chrome around the chart would be. That guess was made when the dialog was a header, a legend and a chart; it never grew with the override banner, the delta row, the third button, the status line or the Temperatures section. Once the real chrome passed the budget the content ran past the dialog's painted background instead of being contained -- 449px past it at 1400x700, measured. Worse than a stale number, it was a feedback loop: a shorter viewport made the dialog *wider*, and _scaleDialogFont derives the font every piece of chrome is sized from off the measured width, so the chrome grew in the same direction as the overflow. So the height budget is gone. The dialog is a flex column bounded by the viewport; the header and legend keep their natural height and everything below them scrolls. The width is still tied to viewport height, but only to keep the chart a sensible size -- nothing depends on guessing the chrome, and being wrong now costs a scrollbar rather than spilled content. The chart keeps its exact aspect ratio deliberately. The width used to be min(96vw, calc((100vh - 168px) * RATIO)), where 168px was a hardcoded guess at how tall the chrome around the chart would be. That guess was made when the dialog was a header, a legend and a chart; it never grew with the override banner, the delta row, the third button, the status line or the Temperatures section. Once the real chrome passed the budget the content ran past the dialog's painted background instead of being contained -- 449px past it at 1400x700, measured. Worse than a stale number, it was a feedback loop: a shorter viewport made the dialog *wider*, and _scaleDialogFont derives the font every piece of chrome is sized from off the measured width, so the chrome grew in the same direction as the overflow. So the height budget is gone. The dialog is a flex column bounded by the viewport; the header and legend keep their natural height and everything below them scrolls. The width is still tied to viewport height, but only to keep the chart a sensible size -- nothing depends on guessing the chrome, and being wrong now costs a scrollbar rather than spilled content.
